Whether this treasure existed or not didn't matter--Shigeo's mind was already spinning stories about vast riches waiting for the right person to claim them.

Yasei
Birthplace - Unknown

Hometown - Plains of Kisaragi

Deity - Hikari

Young Life - Orphaned. Lived well enough with just his bedroll and some kindness. Taken in by the the temples dedicated to The Radiant Lady.

Defining Moment - Found purpose as a field medic, protecting the storehouses from malevolent Yokai. As he ages, his ambition grows to serve a greater purpose. 

Quirk - When Yasei speaks, he speaks with his hands using Common Sign Language. He finds it important to ensure he can communicate with everyone, even if they can't speak or hear. He spends his spare time teaching it to anyone who desires to learn.

Thornbringer
     Thornbringer's people are workers of the land, creators of a cyclopean garden of rare beauty known simply to them as THE GREEN and to outsiders as the Emerald Hills. At the center of their land is a singular tree whose top branches scrape the very sky. It is known only as the "ONE TREE". While the  Goliaths do NOT worship it as a God, they cherish it as the center of their very existence. 
     Once every 10 generations a CHAMPION is chosen who must seek the legendary Well of Life and return with some of its mystic water to bestow another thousand years of blessings upon the ONE TREE. Thornbringer has been chosen as the one to seek The Well this millennia However once visited, the path to the Well is lost and must be found again.  This is his task. His burden. His joy.
     He bears a simple wooden carving of 2 leaves, one light, one dark, curling in on each other with an acorn at the center of each leaf - the symbol of the "Verdant Seeker" his anointed title.

```
chaos nor order to dominate.
```

## Timeline of Shuigari

Timeline of Shuigari

**Year 31-500: Post-Sundering Recovery**

```
? Rebuilding Efforts: Communities focus on survival and reconstruction after the
```
```
Sundering, a cataclysmic event that fractured the land.
```
```
? Rise of Local Leaders: Tribes and city-states emerge, often led by spiritual figures
```
```
interpreting the will of the kami (spirits or gods).
```
```
? Cultural Fragmentation: Diverse responses to the Sundering lead to regional
```
```
differences in language, religion, and governance.
```
```
? Role of Elves: With their longevity, elves become keepers of knowledge and
```
```
pre-Sundering traditions.
```
**Year 500-1,200: The Age of Warring States**

```
? Regional Powers Clash: Daimyo (feudal lords) vie for dominance, forming and breaking
```
```
alliances, with warfare becoming commonplace.
```
```
? Introduction of the Shogunate Concept: Early attempts at centralization arise, led by
```
```
charismatic leaders or alliances.
```
```
? Spiritual Turmoil: Conflicting interpretations of the kami's will lead to fragmented
```
```
religious practices.
```
```
? Role of Long-Lived Races: Elves and dwarves lend their skills to warring factions, with
```
```
elves as advisors or mystics and dwarves as engineers and blacksmiths.
```
```
? Key Cultural Innovations: Distinct architectural styles, martial traditions, and artistic
```
```
expressions emerge.
```
**Year 1,200-1,800: The Rise of the Shogunate**

```
? Unification: A single leader establishes the Shogunate, bringing peace and order
```
```
through military campaigns and subjugation of rival daimyos.
```
```
? Codification of Laws: Legal and social structures are formalized, creating a rigid class
```
```
system.
```
```
? Religious Syncretism: A standardized pantheon emerges, unifying kami worship and
```
```
suppressing chaotic yokai (supernatural entities) influences. The Emperor's role as a
```
```
divine figure solidifies.
```
```
? Role of Long-Lived Races: Elves contribute to cultural refinement, magic, and
```
```
diplomacy, while dwarves thrive in mining and craftsmanship.
```
**Year 1,800-2,100: Cultural Flourishing**


```
? Golden Age: Stability under the Shogunate leads to a flourishing of art, literature,
```
```
philosophy, and mysticism.
```
```
? Magical Advancements: Elves lead efforts in enchanting artifacts, and magic becomes
```
```
more integrated into daily life.
```
```
? Naval Power Emerges: Coastal prefectures develop strong fleets for trade and defense.
```
```
? Friction with Long-Lived Races: Humans become more independent, causing
```
```
resentment or isolationism among some elves. Dwarven focus shifts inward as their
```
```
mines deplete.
```
**Year 2,100-2,300: The Current Era**

```
? Political Decline: The Shogun's authority weakens due to internal struggles and
```
```
external threats.
```
```
? Tensions with the Emperor: Spiritual reformists advocate for greater Imperial authority,
```
```
challenging the Shogunate.
```
```
? Economic Shifts: Merchants and artisans gain wealth, disrupting the traditional
```
```
hierarchy.
```
```
? Elves and Dwarves in the Present: Elves retreat to enclaves as observers or
```
```
knowledge keepers. Dwarves adapt to economic roles using their technical expertise.
```
**Narrative Themes Across the Timeline**

```
? Tradition vs. Change: The interplay between rigid structures and evolving societal
```
```
demands, highlighted by the tension between long-lived and short-lived races.
```
```
? Balance and Duality: Reflected in the relationship between the Emperor (spiritual) and
```
```
Shogunate (political), and between humans and elder races.
```
```
? Conflict and Resilience: Shuigari's ability to rebuild and adapt after the Sundering and
```
```
through centuries of strife.
```
## Prefectures of Shugane

#### Takashima Prefecture

```
? Location and Terrain: Takashima is located in the rugged northern mountains of
```
```
Shugane. The land is defined by its harsh terrain and its role as the island's shield
```
```
against external threats.
```
```
? Leadership: The daimyo of Takashima, a stern and uncompromising warrior, rules from
```
```
the fortress city of Takashima. The daimyo is fiercely loyal to the Shogunate and
```
```
maintains strict adherence to tradition and martial values.
```
#### Settlements

```
? Takashima (Capital) : A fortress city known for its imposing defenses and
```
```
strategic importance.
```

```
? Shimizu: A mountain village renowned for producing skilled blacksmiths and its
```
```
connection to legendary swordsmith lore.
```
```
? Nagano: A forest village known for its masterful archers and their spirit-blessed
```
```
archery techniques.
```
```
? Shirogane: A mining village with rich deposits of precious ores in the Celestial
```
```
Spine mountains.
```
```
? Hagane: A logging village that supplies critical timber for Shugane's military and
```
```
spiritual constructions.
```
```
? Futaba: A fortified town strategically located to guard the northern passes.
```
```
? Culture: The prefecture has a strong martial culture, with a focus on strength, honor,
```
```
and tradition. The daimyo's authority is unchallenged within the prefecture's borders.
```
```
? Strategic Importance: Takashima serves as a buffer against invasions and threats from
```
```
beyond the island. The prefecture's military strength and fortified settlements are crucial
```
```
to the security of Shugane.
```
**Key Points**

```
? Takashima is a mountainous and strategically important prefecture.
```
```
? The daimyo is a powerful and traditional warrior loyal to the Shogunate.
```
```
? The prefecture is known for its skilled blacksmiths, archers, and rich natural resources.
```
```
? The culture is martial and emphasizes strength and honor.
```
```
? Takashima plays a crucial role in defending Shugane from external threats.
```
#### Hikosan Prefecture

Hikosan Prefecture, located in the heart of Shugane, is the political and spiritual core of the

island. It is the seat of the Shogunate, and the Shogun rules from the Imperial Palace in the

capital city of Hikosan. The prefecture is a bustling hub of activity, filled with courtiers, diplomats,

and warriors seeking power and influence within the Shogun's court.

Beyond the capital city, Hikosan Prefecture features several other important settlements:

```
? Takayama: A thriving river town known for its docks and strategic importance as a trade
```
```
and supply hub for the capital.
```
```
? Natsubara: Rich farmlands that produce rice, a staple food for the Shogunate's elite.
```
```
? Kasumi: A small, mysterious village shrouded in mist, where forest spirits are believed
```
```
to dwell.
```
```
? Kameno: A lively market town and a vital link in Hikosan's trade network.
```
```
? Kawagata: A serene riverside village known for its tranquil atmosphere and the
```
```
blessings of river spirits.
```
The Shogun's presence in Hikosan ensures stability and order, but it also raises the stakes for

those involved in the prefecture's governance. The daimyo of Hikosan must navigate the

complexities of the Shogun's court and maintain control over the prefecture while upholding the

integrity of the capital.


## Factions

#### Obsidian Circle

##### Overview

```
? Nature: A clandestine sect operating in Takashima and the surrounding region, devoted
```
```
to the primordial being Tsurogami, the Woven Tyrant.
```
```
? Goal: To unbind Tsurogami from its ancient prison beneath Takashima and usher in a
```
```
new age of perfection.
```
```
? Methods: Diplomacy, espionage, and the summoning of yokai or aberrations to sow
```
```
chaos and weaken the spiritual barriers holding Tsurogami.
```
##### Leadership and Recruitment

```
? Leader: Lord Daizen Kuroda, a charismatic and enigmatic nobleman, serves as the
```
```
public face and spiritual guide.
```
```
? Recruitment: Attracts disillusioned samurai, ambitious nobles, and those seeking power
```
```
or immortality under Tsurogami's new order.
```
##### History

```
? Origins: The Circle emerged during the Age of Sealing, after Tsurogami was imprisoned
```
```
beneath Takashima. Remnants of its followers formed a cult to preserve rituals and
```
```
knowledge for its return.
```
```
? Early Influence: The Circle operated as a secretive cult, infiltrating temples and noble
```
```
courts to sow chaos and weaken the celestial wards.
```
```
? Temporary Defeat: In the early Third Age, the Circle was nearly destroyed by a coalition
```
```
of Shuigari daimyo and kami.
```
```
? Rebirth: Decades ago, Lord Daizen Kuroda rediscovered the Circle's remnants and
```
```
restructured it with a focus on calculated influence.
```
##### Modern Activities

```
? Infiltration: Members hold positions in courts, guilds, and temples, working to
```
```
destabilize key institutions.
```
```
? Relic Hunting: They search for artifacts tied to Tsurogami's sealing.
```
```
? Yokai Manipulation: The Circle binds yokai to their service to spread chaos.
```
```
? Political Machinations: Daizen manipulates political factions into conflict, weakening
```
```
alliances and strengthening the Circle's reach.
```

##### Philosophy

```
? Tsurogami as Necessary Evil: The Circle believes Tsurogami is a force of change and
```
```
that mortals must embrace chaos to achieve perfection.
```
```
? Rejection of Balance: They oppose the traditional Shuigari philosophy of balance and
```
```
harmony.
```
```
? Unity Through Fear: They believe fear of Tsurogami will unify Shuigari under their
```
```
leadership.
```
##### Current Goals

```
? Unsealing Tsurogami: The Circle focuses on completing the Ritual of Unbinding,
```
```
requiring a specific boy's blood, ancient relics, and yokai sacrifices.
```
```
? Undermining Shuigari: They continue to destabilize Shuigari's systems.
```
```
? Securing Allies: Daizen seeks to recruit powerful individuals to the Circle's cause.
```
##### Legacy and Impact

The Obsidian Circle has had a lasting impact on Shuigari, influencing its history through their

philosophy of destruction and rebirth. Under Daizen's leadership, they are closer than ever to

achieving their goal of unleashing Tsurogami and reshaping the world.

#### Lord Daizen Kuroda: A Charismatic Noble with a Dark Secret

##### Public Persona

```
? Appearance: Lord Daizen Kuroda presents himself as a wise and composed figure. He
```
```
has graying hair, subtle wrinkles, and piercing black eyes that seem to see through
```
```
people.
```
```
? Clothing: He wears elegant, dark indigo robes adorned with gold trim and understated
```
```
jewelry.
```
```
? Personality: He exudes serenity, speaks in a soothing voice, and uses carefully chosen
```
```
words to inspire trust and loyalty. He often communicates through parables, offering
```
```
seemingly wise advice that subtly manipulates those around him.
```
```
? Reputation: He is highly respected in Takashima, known for mediating disputes,
```
```
strengthening alliances, and supporting philanthropic endeavors.
```
##### True Role as Sect Leader

```
? Private Personality: Beneath the calm exterior lies a ruthless and ambitious individual
```
```
driven by the belief that unleashing the entity Tsurogami is necessary for a new era. He
```
```
justifies his actions as those of a visionary who transcends conventional morality.
```
```
? Abilities:
```

```
? Master Manipulator: He expertly employs his charm, wealth, and political
```
```
influence to conceal the Obsidian Circle's activities.
```
```
? Psionic Talents: He possesses psionic abilities, including Suggestion, Dominate
```
```
Person, and Detect Thoughts, which he uses to control others.
```
```
? Aberration Tamer: He commands two Umbrawraiths, powerful aberrations
```
```
bound to him through Tsurogami's influence.
```
##### Umbrawraiths

```
? Appearance: These large aberrations resemble eyeless panthers with shadowy tendrils
```
```
extending from their spines. Their forms shimmer like liquid shadow, and their claws drip
```
```
with a corrosive substance.
```
```
? Abilities:
```
```
? Shadow Phase: They can move through solid objects as if they were
```
```
incorporeal.
```
```
? Psychic Shriek: They emit a devastating psychic attack that stuns and terrifies
```
```
creatures within its range.
```
```
? Eclipse Bind: They can lash out with tendrils, restraining targets and draining
```
```
their life force.
```
**Overall, Lord Daizen Kuroda is a complex and formidable villain who hides his true**

**nature behind a mask of benevolence and wisdom.** He serves as the leader of the Obsidian

Circle, a cult seeking to unleash the entity Tsurogami upon the world, and he will use any means

necessary to achieve his goals.

##### Tsurogami

Tsurogami, also known as the Woven Tyrant, is an ancient entity of chaos in the world of

Shuigari. Here's what we know about it:

```
? Nature and Origins: Tsurogami predates the gods and embodies the unraveling of
```
```
mortal will and the reweaving of existence according to its own alien designs.
```
```
? Sealed Prison: Tsurogami's essence is imprisoned beneath Takashima, bound by a
```
```
complex web of celestial wards maintained by the balance of the Three Moons
```
```
(Dendaro, Intara, Tsukuyomi).
```
```
? Manifestation:
```
```
? Appearance: When manifested, Tsurogami appears as a vast, shapeless entity
```
```
resembling a monstrous spider woven from threads of ichor and shadow. Its form
```
```
constantly shifts, sprouting grotesque eyes, limbs, and mouths at random.
```
```
? Voice: Tsurogami speaks in overlapping tones, and each word carries hypnotic
```
```
power, compelling mortals to submit.
```

#### The Grey Tide

```
? Nature: The Grey Tide is a group of pirates and smugglers operating around the
```
```
Takashima region of Shuigari.
```
```
? Primary Operations:
```
```
? Smuggling: They use hidden coves along the coastline to smuggle contraband
```
```
such as Frostiron, stolen artifacts, and enchanted items.
```
```
? Piracy: They frequently target merchant ships traveling along the Silver Road's
```
```
maritime routes.
```
```
? Local Reputation:
```
```
? Among Villagers: The Grey Tide has a mixed reputation among coastal
```
```
villagers. Some view them as folk heroes who offer protection from other pirates
```
```
or bandits, while others resent their exploitation.
```
```
? With Nobles: Nobles in Takashima City see the Grey Tide as a nuisance
```
```
disrupting trade and resource control.
```
```
? Recent Activity:
```
```
? Increased Tension: The Grey Tide recently raided a shipment of enchanted
```
```
blades, leading to heightened military presence in the region. Rumors suggest
```
```
they may have intercepted one of Hideo's final creations.
```
```
? Spiritual Disturbances: The Grey Tide angered a sea kami during a smuggling
```
```
operation, causing increased yokai activity near the coast.
```
##### Potential Hooks for the Campaign:

```
? Infiltration: A Grey Tide captain may attend Hideo's funeral in disguise to gather
```
```
information or seek potential allies.
```
```
? Information Brokers: The Grey Tide may have information about stolen artifacts tied to
```
```
Hideo's legacy.
```
```
? Secret Alliances: A covert meeting between a Takashima noble and a Grey Tide
```
```
representative could suggest hidden alliances or betrayals.
```
```
? Past Connections: A low-ranking member of the Grey Tide was once helped by Hideo,
```
```
offering a potential connection to the group.
```
## Curiosities and Oddities

#### Overview

```
? Name: The Twilight Carnival of Thousand Lanterns (Senr? no Tasogare)
```
```
? Nature: A mysterious traveling festival appearing at seasonal changes or auspicious
```
```
celestial events, blending tradition and the supernatural.
```
```
? Reputation: Known for dazzling performances, challenging games, and hidden secrets
```
```
and mysteries, with rumors of yokai involvement.
```

#### Physical Layout

```
? Appearance: Ephemeral, with silk-draped tents and pavilions, adorned with countless
```
```
paper lanterns.
```
```
? Sensory Details: The air is filled with the aroma of roasted sweet potatoes, soy-glazed
```
```
rice crackers, and sake, accompanied by the sounds of flutes, shamisen, and taiko
```
```
drums.
```
```
? Key Features:
```
```
? Torii Gate Entrance: A symbolic gateway into another realm.
```
```
? Lantern Boulevard: A central path illuminated by a canopy of colorful paper
```
```
lanterns.
```
```
? The Sakura Stage: A central stage built around a perpetually blooming cherry
```
```
blossom tree, hosting nightly performances.
```
```
? The Twilight Pavilion: A private tent housing the carnival's leader, Mistress
```
```
Tsukiyo.
```
#### Notable NPCs

```
? Mistress Tsukiyo: The enigmatic leader, possibly a kami or yokai, offering cryptic
```
```
advice and challenges.
```
```
? Hajime the Flame Dancer: A skilled fire performer with a mischievous streak, hinting at
```
```
unsettling changes within the carnival.
```
```
? Kohana the Fox-Masked Girl: A cheerful game master overseeing various carnival
```
```
games, concealing secrets beneath her playful demeanor.
```
#### Activities and Atmosphere

```
? Performances: Traditional music, acrobatics, and kabuki-style plays on the Sakura
```
```
Stage.
```
```
? Games and Challenges: Ring tosses, shooting galleries, goldfish scooping, and other
```
```
games with potential prizes or curses.
```
```
? Secrets and Mysteries: Rumors of yokai involvement and spiritual dangers lurking
```
```
beneath the festivities.
```
#### Overall Impression

The Twilight Carnival is a captivating and immersive experience, where the lines between reality

and the spirit world blur. It offers a blend of entertainment, cultural immersion, and hidden

dangers, making it an intriguing setting for adventures and exploration.


## Flora and Fauna of Shuigari

The rich tapestry of Shuigari's landscapes is woven with a diverse array of flora and fauna, each

playing an integral role in the delicate balance of the natural world and the cultural fabric of the

land. While the following lists offer a glimpse into the unique plants and creatures that inhabit

Shuigari, they are by no means exhaustive. Countless other species, both known and unknown,

await discovery in the hidden corners and depths of this mystical realm.

#### Flora of Shuigari

Shuigari boasts a diverse and vibrant array of plant life, each with unique properties and cultural

significance. From the healing Jadeleaf Fern found in the sacred forests of Hisui no Mori to the

warmth-retaining Frostiron Moss used by those braving the cold climates of Furenhoku, the flora

of Shuigari plays an essential role in the lives of its inhabitants.Table of Unique Plants

#### Fauna of Shuigari

Shuigari is teeming with a diverse array of creatures, both mundane and magical, each playing

a unique role in the ecosystem and culture of the land. From the mischievous Kitsuhana foxes

that serve as messengers of the kami to the majestic Crimson Fang tigers that roam the

Furenhoku mountains, the fauna of Shuigari is as captivating as it is vital to the world's delicate

balance.

## Food and Drink of Shuigari

Shuigari boasts a rich and diverse culinary tradition, influenced by its unique geography, climate,

and cultural heritage. From the hearty stews of the mountainous regions to the fresh seafood

dishes of the coastal towns, Shuigari's cuisine offers a taste of the land's soul. Accompanying

these delectable dishes is an equally varied selection of drinks, ranging from refreshing herbal


teas to potent rice wines. Whether you seek a comforting meal after a long day's journey or a

refreshing beverage to enjoy with friends, Shuigari has something to tantalize your taste buds.

This section delves into the culinary delights of Shuigari, exploring both common and regional

specialties. We'll also introduce you to the various drinks that quench the thirst of its inhabitants,

from traditional favorites to unique local concoctions. So, sit back, relax, and prepare to embark

on a culinary adventure through the vibrant land of Shuigari!

## Calendar of Shuigari

The Shuigari Calendar: A Detailed Overview

**Structure:**

```
? Year Length: 12 months, each with 30 days.
```
```
? Weeks: Each month is divided into three ten-day weeks (decads).
```
```
? Seasonal Alignment: Seasons transition smoothly, influenced by lunar cycles and the
```
```
will of the kami.
```
```
? Lunar Influence: The three moons, Dendaro, Intara, and Tsukuyomi, guide festivals and
```
```
spiritual observances.
```
**Months of the Year:**

```
? Spring:
```
```
? Kaminatsu (Spring's Blessing): Renewal, beginnings, blessings from the Earth
```
```
Kami.
```
```
? Haranami (Waves of Chaos): Fertility, unpredictable winds, rituals for safe
```
```
planting.
```
```
? Sakuraji (Cherry Bloom): Cherry blossoms, festivals of beauty, harmony, and
```
```
prosperity.
```
```
? Summer:
```
```
? Tenka (Heaven's Fire): Strength, ambition, offerings to the Fire Kami for good
```
```
fortune.
```
```
? Hisakata (Endless Sky): Wisdom, exploration, festivals honoring celestial kami
```
```
and travelers.
```
```
? Kaij? (Ocean's Heart): Unity, abundance, oceanic rituals to honor the Water
```
```
Kami.
```
```
? Autumn:
```
```
? Momiji (Maple Leaf): Gratitude, harvest, offerings to ancestors and spirits of the
```
```
fields.
```
```
? Tsukinami (Moonlight Path): Reflection, preparation for winter, quiet prayers to
```
```
the Moon Kami.
```
```
? Kuroame (Black Rain): Mourning, remembrance, cleansing.
```
```
? Winter:
```
```
? Yukizora (Snow Sky): Purity, stillness, sacred snow ceremonies, renewal rituals.
```

```
? Fubuki (Winter Storm): Resilience, challenges, enduring the storms of life and
```
```
season.
```
```
? Setsunai (Frozen Heart): Endings, spiritual renewal, reflecting on the year's
```
```
trials.
```
```
Key Observances and Festivals:
```
```
? Spring Equinox (1st Day of Kaminatsu): Renewal, planting blessings, honoring the
```
```
Earth Kami.
```
```
? Cherry Blossom Feast (Mid-Sakuraji): Celebrating beauty and fleeting moments of
```
```
peace and harmony.
```
```
? Summer Solstice (Mid-Hisakata): Fire Kami rituals, celestial observations, trials of
```
```
strength.
```
```
? Harvest Festival (1st Day of Momiji): Gratitude ceremonies to ancestors and spirits,
```
```
marked by feasting.
```
```
? Autumn Equinox (Mid-Tsukinami): Quiet reflection and preparation for winter;
```
```
moon-viewing rituals.
```
```
? Tsukuyomi's Night (Variable): Mystical event tied to spiritual energy; omens and
```
```
powerful rituals.
```
```
? Winter Solstice (1st Day of Yukizora): Snow Kami blessings for purity and resilience;
```
```
village feasts.
```
```
Additional Notes:
```
```
? Sacred Days: Each month has a Sacred Day dedicated to a specific kami or natural
```
```
force.
```
```
? Lunar Influence on Magic: The phases of the moons can affect magic and spiritual
```
```
events.
```
```
? Festivals Tied to Kami/Yokai: Numerous festivals celebrate and honor specific kami
```
```
and yokai throughout the year.
```
```
In-Game Use:
```
```
? Immersion: The calendar provides a rich backdrop for storytelling and roleplaying.
```
```
? Quest Opportunities: Seasonal events and festivals can inspire quests and adventures.
```
```
? Strategic Gameplay: The lunar cycles can influence magical abilities and create
```
```
strategic opportunities.
```

### Sousteel: Legendary Weapons of Shuigari

```
? Definition: Soulsteel Blades are rare, magical weapons infused with the essence of the
```
```
wielder's soul, representing the pinnacle of Master Hideo Nakata's craftsmanship.
```
```
? Key Characteristics:
```
```
? Material: Soulsteel is a mystical alloy forged from rare ores (including
```

```
kami-blessed Frostiron) found in the Celestial Spine mountains and imbued with
```
```
spiritual energy during the forging process.
```
```
? Attunement: Each blade is uniquely bound to the individual for whom it was
```
```
crafted.
```
```
? Resonance: The blade responds to the wielder's emotions, intentions, and
```
```
spiritual state.
```
```
? Magical Properties: The blade's appearance reflects the wielder's soul, and it
```
```
manifests unique powers tied to their spiritual traits.
```
```
? Legacy Bond: Upon the wielder's death, the blade becomes inert and can only
```
```
be reawakened under extraordinary circumstances.
```
? **Forging Process:**

```
? Soul Binding Ritual: The smith and the intended wielder meditate together,
```
```
allowing the smith to glimpse the wielder's soul and imbue a fragment of their
```
```
spiritual energy into the molten Soulsteel.
```
```
? Forging Technique: The blade is shaped with physical tools and wordsmithing
```
```
magic.
```
```
? Blessing by Kami: The finished blade is blessed by a kami (usually
```
```
Tsuchi-no-Megumi), sealing its bond to the wielder.
```
? **Notable Soulsteel Blades:**

```
? "Yureihikari" (Ghostlight): Wielded by a samurai seeking redemption, it reveals
```
```
hidden enemies and cuts through incorporeal beings.
```
```
? "Shinseiken" (Sacred Flame): Wielded by a warrior devoted to protecting the
```
```
weak, it burns with holy fire, harming yokai and purging curses.
```
```
? "Kuragetsu" (Dark Moon): Wielded by the Twice Cursed Samurai, it amplifies
```
```
the wielder's inner turmoil and strength at the cost of spiritual torment.
```
? **In-Game Mechanics and Roleplay Hooks:**

```
? Attunement and Use: Soulsteel Blades require attunement and can only be
```
```
wielded by their intended owner.
```
```
? Story Seeds: Recovering a lost blade, forging a new blade, or dealing with a
```
```
blade's curse can drive quests and character development.
```

#### Nagano

###### Of course! Here's the content formatted for improved readability and clarity, while

###### retaining the original information:

##### Rewards

###### A central figure or system for reporting quests and earning rewards is essential.

###### ? Quest Giver: Mayor Hiroshi Takeda oversees adventurer rewards. He

###### delegates through a steward, often found in Takeda Manor.

###### ? Adventurer's Guild (The Path of Blades): A small, informal guild hall where

###### adventurers report completed tasks and find new ones. The guildmaster, Kana

###### Yoshiro, is a retired swordswoman.

###### ? Religious Rewards: The Temple of the Three Moons rewards spiritual services

###### or rare relics for quests tied to the kami or yokai disturbances.

##### Rest

###### Options for safe lodging and rest are diverse, giving players flexibility and roleplay

###### opportunities.

###### ? The Jade Lantern: A mid-tier inn with modest accommodations and a lively

###### tavern. Owned by Yuto and Aiko Mori , who gossip about local happenings.

###### ? Temple Sanctuary: The Temple of the Three Moons offers free lodging for the

###### devout or those serving the community. Simple mats and shared quarters.

###### ? Local Hospitality: Villagers sometimes open their homes to travelers. Aya the

###### Weaver or Taro the Hunter might host adventurers in exchange for small favors

###### or news.


###### ? Camping: Near the Frostwood Outpost, adventurers can camp safely under

###### militia protection.

##### Resupply

###### A variety of shops and services allow players to trade, sell, or restock.

###### ? Market District: Vendors offer general goods, local produce, and small magical

###### trinkets.

###### ? Artisan's Row

###### ? Haruto's Forge: A blacksmith inspired by Master Hideo, Haruto crafts

###### weapons and tools with care.

###### ? Ayame's Apothecary: Specializing in herbal remedies and basic potions,

###### Ayame is known for her knowledge of Frostwood flora.

###### ? Special Items: Players can commission unique items, such as charms blessed

###### by the temple priests or custom blades forged from rare materials.

##### Research

###### Opportunities to gather information and investigate the world's mysteries.

###### ? The Temple Library: A modest collection of scrolls on local lore, kami, and

###### yokai. The head priest, Reiko Matsuda , can guide research efforts.

###### ? The Storyteller's Hearth: A small alehouse where locals gather to share tales.

###### Renji Sato , a former bandit, knows much about hidden paths and old legends.

###### ? Village Elders: Knowledge of the Frostwood and its peculiarities is passed down

###### orally. Villagers like Yumi Harada , a retired hunter, can provide insights.


##### Recreation

###### Unique leisure opportunities immerse players in the culture and life of Nagano.

###### ? Seasonal Festivals:

###### ? The Spring Equinox Festival happens during the adventure, with music,

###### dances, and ceremonial offerings to the Earth Kami.

###### ? Combat Training:

###### ? The Frostwood Dojo offers sparring matches and lessons. Run by Riku

###### Matsunaga , it's a good way to spend downtime.

###### ? Gambling and Games:

###### ? The Jade Lantern Tavern hosts dice games, card games, and storytelling

###### competitions.

###### ? Artisan Workshops:

###### ? Players can participate in pottery, weaving, or smithing as a creative

###### break.

##### Key NPCs of Nagano

###### ? Mayor Hiroshi Takeda: Leader of Nagano, a calm and pragmatic administrator

###### with deep respect for Hideo's legacy.

###### ? Kana Yoshiro: Guildmaster of the adventurer's guild, a sharp-witted retired

###### swordswoman who knows the political and spiritual undercurrents of the region.

###### ? Reiko Matsuda: Head priest of the Temple of the Three Moons, who serves as a

###### spiritual guide and historian.

###### ? Haruto the Blacksmith: Inspired by Hideo, Haruto is determined to uphold the

###### smithing traditions passed down to him.


###### ? Renji Sato: Former bandit indebted to Hideo, Renji now works as a guide and

###### informant, though his past still haunts him.

###### Mayor Hiroshi Takeda

```
? Title: Mayor of Nagano
```
```
? Race: Human
```
```
? Age: 56
```
```
? Appearance: A well-built man with a weathered face, gray-streaked hair tied in a simple
```
```
bun, and sharp, observant eyes. He wears plain but dignified robes, preferring function
```
```
over ostentation, and always carries a writing case with him.
```
```
? Personality: Pragmatic and diplomatic, Hiroshi is deeply invested in Nagano's welfare
```
```
and the safety of its people. Known for his even temper and quiet resolve, he
```
```
approaches conflicts with careful consideration. A former samurai who served under a
```
```
minor lord in his youth, he now devotes himself to administration, though his warrior
```
```
instincts remain sharp.
```
Role in Nagano

```
? Hiroshi Takeda is the steady hand that keeps Nagano functioning amidst growing
```
```
tensions in Shuigari. His background as a samurai lends him respect among the village
```
```
militia and samurai retainers, while his genuine care for the common folk has earned him
```
```
the trust of Nagano's villagers.
```
```
? Hiroshi has a strong connection to the legacy of Master Hideo Nakata, having been an
```
```
admirer and beneficiary of Hideo's wisdom. Hideo once forged a katana for Hiroshi when
```
```
he was a young samurai, a blade that Hiroshi still treasures, though he rarely wields it.
```
Relationship with Lord Ichiro Kagemura

```
? Hiroshi's relationship with Lord Kagemura is one of necessity, mutual respect, and thinly
```
```
veiled tension. As Nagano falls within Kagemura's jurisdiction, Hiroshi must navigate the
```
```
noble's ambitions while protecting the town's autonomy.
```
```
? Dependence on Lord Kagemura: Nagano relies on Kagemura for military support
```
```
against external threats, such as yokai incursions from the Frostwood and bandit raids.
```
```
Kagemura's influence also affects trade routes, which are vital to Nagano's economy.
```
```
Hiroshi often has to carefully negotiate to secure fair treatment for the town.
```
```
? Hiroshi's Disapproval: Hiroshi respects Kagemura's capability as a leader but quietly
```
```
disapproves of the lord's cunning and manipulative tendencies. Kagemura's interest in
```
```
consolidating power worries Hiroshi, who fears the lord may exploit Nagano's resources
```
```
or position for political gain.
```
```
? Mutual Respect: Despite their differences, Kagemura recognizes Hiroshi's skill as a
```
```
leader and values his ability to manage Nagano effectively. Hiroshi, in turn, respects
```
```
Kagemura's military acumen and his ability to maintain stability in a volatile region, even
```

```
if his methods are questionable.
```
```
? Tensions over the Funeral: Kagemura's attendance at Hideo's funeral brings added
```
```
pressure on Hiroshi to maintain the event's sanctity while managing the subtle political
```
```
games among the nobles. Hiroshi suspects Kagemura's motives for attending are more
```
```
political than personal, and he worries the lord may attempt to manipulate the attendees,
```
```
including Lady Hana Arashi, for his own benefit.
```
Hiroshi's Motivations

```
? Hiroshi seeks to safeguard Nagano's independence and honor the legacy of Master
```
```
Hideo by ensuring the town thrives as a place of craftsmanship and cultural significance.
```
```
His primary goals include:
```
```
? Protecting the People: Ensuring Nagano's safety from yokai and bandit threats,
```
```
often negotiating directly with Kagemura's samurai for aid.
```
```
? Honoring Hideo's Legacy: Preserving the town's reputation as a center for
```
```
artisanal excellence and spiritual harmony.
```
```
? Navigating Politics: Balancing Nagano's autonomy while maintaining a working
```
```
relationship with Kagemura and other regional powers.
```
Potential Hooks for the Campaign

```
? A Test of Loyalties: Kagemura pressures Hiroshi to offer more resources or militia
```
```
support for a campaign against rival daimyo, creating conflict within the town.
```
```
? Hiroshi's Old Blade: Hiroshi's katana, forged by Hideo, becomes a target of interest for
```
```
Kagemura or other factions seeking to possess a weapon tied to the revered smith.
```
```
? Political Intrigue: Kagemura uses the funeral as an opportunity to influence Hiroshi,
```
```
possibly offering him a more prominent role in regional politics---or threatening
```
```
consequences if he resists.
```
```
? An Uneasy Alliance: A crisis forces Hiroshi and Kagemura to cooperate directly, with the
```
```
players caught between their differing philosophies and approaches.
```
##### Unique Features of Nagano

###### ? The Whispering Glade: A serene cemetery outside Nagano, where Hideo is

###### buried. It's a site of spiritual power, tied to both the kami and yokai of the

###### Frostwood.

###### ? The Frostwood Outpost: Manned by Nagano's militia, this outpost protects

###### travelers and merchants while monitoring the Frostwood's unpredictable dangers.

###### ? The Weaver's Market: Held every ten-day, this market attracts merchants from

###### nearby villages, offering exotic goods and opportunities for intrigue.

##### How These Fit Into the Campaign

###### ? Worldbuilding: These tasks immerse players in the daily life of Nagano and

###### connect them to key NPCs.


###### ? Foreshadowing: Some quests, like "The Stolen Relic" or "Yokai Infestation," hint

###### at larger threats, such as the Obsidian Circle's activities.

###### ? Downtime Activities: Festival preparations, artisan aid, or patrols provide

###### opportunities for roleplay and character development.

###### ? Adventure Hooks: Tasks like "Missing Hunter" or "Merchant's Caravan in Peril"

###### can evolve into longer adventures that lead into the overarching plot.

## Frostwood Outpost

**Location and Purpose.** The Frostwood Outpost is a small military structure on the northern

edge of Nagano, marking the boundary between civilization and the encroaching Frostwood. It


serves as a defensive bastion and a crucial waypoint for travelers venturing into the perilous

forest.

#### Key Features

```
? Watchtower: A wooden tower offering a panoramic view of the Frostwood and
```
```
surrounding areas.
```
```
? Barracks: Housing for up to 20 militia members, equipped with basic amenities and a
```
```
small armory.
```
```
? Palisade Walls: Sturdy wooden walls with sharpened stakes for defense.
```
```
? Gatehouse: The main entrance, guarded around the clock.
```
```
? Supply Depot: Stores food, medical supplies, and emergency provisions.
```
```
? Shrine to the Earth Kami: A place of worship for the militia, dedicated to
```
```
Tsuchi-no-Megumi for protection and stability.
```
#### Personnel

```
? Captain Shiro Tanabe: A seasoned half-orc leader, practical, disciplined, and deeply
```
```
protective of his soldiers and the town.
```
```
? Lieutenant Akari Fujita: A skilled elven scout and second-in-command, known for her
```
```
tactical planning, dry humor, and extensive knowledge of the Frostwood.
```
```
? Militia Members: A rotating team of 12-20 individuals, each with specific roles like
```
```
sentry duty, patrols, and quartermaster tasks.
```
#### Roles of the Outpost

```
? Defense and Patrols: Regular patrols are conducted to ensure the safety of travelers
```
```
and merchants. The outpost acts as the first line of defense against yokai and bandit
```
```
incursions.
```
```
? Traveler Assistance: The outpost provides guidance, warnings, and temporary shelter
```
```
or supplies to those venturing into the Frostwood.
```
```
? Intelligence Gathering: Scouts gather information about yokai activity, bandit
```
```
movements, and unusual occurrences in the Frostwood, relaying it to Nagano's
```
```
leadership.
```
```
? Emergency Response: Serves as a staging ground for the militia in case of attacks on
```
```
Nagano or nearby settlements.
```
#### Ongoing Issues and Opportunities for Players:

```
? Increased Yokai Activity: Yokai are venturing closer to the outpost, especially during
```
```
Tsukuyomi's influence, suggesting a spiritual imbalance in the Frostwood.
```
```
? Missing Scouts: Two scouts have vanished, and Captain Tanabe offers a reward for
```
```
their safe return or information about their fate.
```
```
? Bandit Problem: A bandit group led by Jiro the Thorn is ambushing travelers, and the
```

```
outpost struggles to locate them.
```
```
? Spiritual Disruption: Strange phenomena near the shrine suggest a disruption, and
```
```
Reiko Matsuda from the Temple of the Three Moons might seek player assistance.
```
```
? The Raven Yokai's Message: A sighting of a massive raven yokai on the day of Hideo's
```
```
funeral raises questions about its significance.
```
#### Role in the Campaign

```
? Launch Point: A starting location for missions into the Frostwood, providing supplies
```
```
and information.
```
```
? Hub of Intrigue: NPCs may reveal crucial details about yokai disturbances or the
```
```
Obsidian Circle's activities.
```
```
? Moral Dilemma: Players might face choices about how to assist the outpost while
```
```
considering the broader needs of Nagano and the prophecy.
```

# The Frostwood

**General Dangers:** The Frostwood is depicted as a perilous and mysterious location, filled with

various threats such as yokai, bandits, and corrupted spirits.

```
? Specific Encounters: The document lists several specific encounters that can occur
```
```
within the Frostwood, including:
```
```
? The Frostwood Guardian: A massive stone guardian that protects the forest.
```
```
? Spirit Fog and Yokai Ambush: Dense fog that can disorient travelers and
```
```
provide cover for kappa yokai attacks.
```
```
? Corrupted Shrine: Defiled shrines to the Earth Kami that summon shadow
```
```
elementals.
```
```
? Hungry Jorogumo: A spider yokai that spins webs and lures prey with the cries
```
```
of trapped travelers.
```
```
? Bandit Lair: Hideouts for bandits who ambush travelers.
```
```
? Phantom Stag: A spectral stag that leads to an ancient grove guarded by a
```
```
dryad.
```
```
? Pack of Shadow Wolves: Coordinated packs of shadow wolves that hunt in the
```
```
forest.
```
```
? Whispering Tree: A sentient tree with riddles and treant guardians.
```
```
? Haunting Specters: Specters of fallen warriors seeking vengeance.
```
```
? Raven Yokai and Minions: A mischievous raven yokai accompanied by giant
```
```
ravens.
```
```
? Planar Rift: Tears in reality that release chaotic entities.
```
```
? The Frostwood's Call: An abandoned Soulsteel forge containing a fragment of
```
```
Soulsteel and a guardian spirit.
```
```
? Spiritual Significance: The presence of shrines to the Earth Kami and the mention of a
```
```
guardian spirit suggest that the Frostwood holds spiritual significance. However, the
```
```
corrupted shrines and the presence of yokai and specters also indicate a disruption of
```
```
this spiritual balance.
```
Overall, the Frostwood is portrayed as a place of both natural and supernatural dangers, where

adventurers may face physical and spiritual challenges. The forest also seems to be affected by

some form of spiritual corruption or imbalance, which may be a key element in the overall story

or campaign setting.


## The Twice Cursed Samurai

This adventure blends high fantasy mystery, old-school exploration, and mythic confrontation,

set in the fragmented world of Shuigari. The players are drawn into the Ryuseki Peaks, a

treacherous mountain range shrouded in myth, to uncover the truth behind Master Hideo's

death and confront the enigmatic Twice-Cursed Samurai, who resides in a labyrinthine domain

deep within the peaks.

#### Adventure Overview

In The Twice-Cursed Blade, the characters are drawn into the mystery of Master Hideo Nakata's

death and the strange circumstances surrounding his funeral. As they uncover the truth, they

must journey into the treacherous Ryuseki Peaks to confront the enigmatic Twice-Cursed

Samurai, a tragic and dangerous figure whose actions hold the key to Hideo's sacrifice. Along

the way, the party will explore an ancient forest alive with spiritual forces, navigate a labyrinthine

stronghold, and face trials of honor, resolve, and survival.

#### The Twice Cursed Samurai

```
? A Tragic Figure: The Twice-Cursed Samurai is a legendary figure in Shuigari, shrouded
```
```
in mystery and burdened by a double curse. Their true name and past are unknown, lost
```
```
to the ages.
```
```
? Cursed by Blood: The first curse is tied to their bloodline, marked by a visible scar
```
```
across their face. This curse is said to bring misfortune and suffering upon those around
```
```
them.
```
```
? Cursed by Deed: The second curse is a consequence of a terrible act committed in the
```
```
past, the details of which remain a mystery. This curse is said to have twisted their soul
```
```
and bound them to a spectral realm.
```
```
? Spectral Domain: The Twice-Cursed Samurai resides in a labyrinthine domain within
```
```
the Ryuseki Peaks, a realm woven from their regrets and sorrow. This domain is filled
```
```
with illusions, traps, and spectral guardians, reflecting the Samurai's inner turmoil.
```
```
? Motivation: The Samurai's primary motivation is to break free from their dual curses and
```
```
find solace. They may seek the party's help to achieve this goal, offering cryptic
```
```
guidance or assistance in exchange for their aid.
```
##### Origins

The Twice-Cursed Samurai's origins are shrouded in mystery, with various tales and legends

offering fragmented glimpses into their past. Here are some of the most common theories:


```
? A Fallen Warrior: Some believe the Samurai was once a noble warrior who committed a
```
```
grave sin, leading to their double curse and exile.
```
```
? A Vengeful Spirit: Others claim they are the vengeful spirit of a wronged individual,
```
```
seeking to right a past injustice.
```
```
? A Guardian of Secrets: A few legends suggest the Samurai is a guardian of ancient
```
```
secrets, bound to their spectral realm to protect them from those who seek to misuse
```
```
them.
```
The truth behind the Twice-Cursed Samurai's origins remains elusive, adding to the intrigue and

mystery surrounding this enigmatic figure.

#### Nagano Village and the Funeral

The adventure begins in the village of Nagano, where the funeral of Master Hideo Nakata

Master Hideo introduces the party to the story's central mystery. The characters hear whispers

of the Twice-Cursed Samurai and strange circumstances surrounding Hideo's death.

Investigating the village and Hideo's forge reveals his connection to a hidden family and his

ultimate sacrifice. The players also encounter the jackals, mysterious emissaries of the Samurai

who challenge and provoke them.

#### Hisui no Mori (Jade Forest)

The characters journey into the magical and treacherous Jade Forest, the first step in reaching

the Ryuseki Peaks. The forest is alive with shifting paths, playful yokai, and sacred shrines

where the characters can commune with kami for guidance. The players encounter traps,

puzzles, and hostile spirits, testing their ability to work together and honor the forest's spirits.

Along the way, the jackals continue to toy with the party, offering cryptic riddles and warnings.

#### The Threshold of the Ryuseki Peaks

At the forest's edge, the players reach the Threshold of the Ryuseki Peaks, an ancient gateway

guarded by a spectral warrior. Here, they must pass a trial of skill or resolve to enter the

Samurai's domain. Beyond the gate lies the labyrinthine stronghold, a surreal and disorienting

realm shaped by the Samurai's will.

#### The Labyrinth of the Twice-Cursed Samurai

The adventure culminates in the Ryuseki Peaks, where the players navigate a twisting maze of

shifting stone and spirit-infused glass. The labyrinth's challenges include illusions, traps, and

encounters with yokai guardians. In the Throne Room of the Twice-Cursed Samurai, the party

must face the Samurai in an honorable duel that blends combat with spiritual and emotional

stakes. The Samurai's defeat or surrender reveals the truth about Hideo's sacrifice and the

Samurai's role in protecting Hideo's hidden legacy.


#### Aftermath and Rewards

The Truth Unveiled: The players learn of Hideo's connection to the concubine and her son,

gaining insight into his selfless act.

The Cursed Blade: The Twice-Cursed Samurai's weapon, though dangerous, carries immense

power and could aid the players in future challenges.

A New Ally: If the Samurai survives, they may pledge themselves to the players' cause, offering

cryptic guidance or aid in battles to come.

#### Themes and Tone

**Honor and Sacrifice.** Hideo's death and the Samurai's curse are reminders of the cost of duty

and the legacy of selflessness.

**Mystery and Exploration.** The journey through the forest and peaks mirrors the players' search

for the truth.

**Old-School Dungeon Design.** The labyrinthine nature of the Samurai's domain invites

problem-solving and creative thinking.

#### Adventure Hooks for The Twice-Cursed Blade

Here are some adventure hooks for The Twice-Cursed Blade to draw players into the mystery

surrounding Master Hideo's death and the journey to confront the Twice-Cursed Samurai. Each

hook presents a unique motivation for players to engage with the story, providing flexibility

based on their character backgrounds and the party dynamic.

##### Honoring a Master's Legacy

Master Hideo was a revered smith and sage, known for his unmatched skill in forging Soulsteel

blades and his unwavering dedication to aiding others. Each player has a personal reason to

honor Hideo's memory:

```
? Perhaps he crafted a weapon or tool for them in the past.
```
```
? Maybe he offered wisdom that changed the course of their life.
```
**Motivation.** Attending the funeral allows the players to pay their respects, but whispers of foul

play and a strange emissary at the ceremony compel them to investigate. What could have led

to Hideo's death by the hand of the Twice-Cursed Samurai?

##### A Debt of Honor

One of the players (or someone close to them) owes a life-debt to Master Hideo. Perhaps Hideo

defended them from a threat, repaired an irreplaceable item for them, or helped their family in a

time of need.


**Motivation.** With his death, the players feel duty-bound to ensure his passing was honorable

and to protect his legacy. When the Twice-Cursed Samurai's jackals deliver their cryptic

message at the funeral, the party's oath to repay the debt drives them to investigate the

Samurai and their connection to Hideo.

##### Protecting the Boy

The players notice a child being protected by a veiled woman at the funeral, seemingly avoiding

attention. Rumors among the mourners suggest the boy is Hideo's grandson and that he may

be in danger following the master's death.

**Motivation.** When the players learn of jackals from the Ryuseki Peaks lurking near Nagano,

they suspect the child may be a target. Their investigation leads them to uncover Hideo's true

connection to the boy and the role of the Twice-Cursed Samurai in his death.

##### The Call of the Cursed Blade

The Twice-Cursed Samurai's blade is a weapon of legend, said to have been forged in part by

Master Hideo himself. Rumors claim the blade has the power to sever a soul and bind it in ways

few can comprehend.

**Motivation.** Hearing of Hideo's death at the hands of the Samurai drives the players to seek the

truth. Was Hideo's death a betrayal, or is there something more to this fateful act? Tracking the

blade and its wielder to the Ryuseki Peaks becomes their obsession.

##### A Rivalry Rekindled

One of the players has a personal rivalry with the Twice-Cursed Samurai, either due to a shared

history or a grudge involving their cursed blade. The Samurai's appearance at Master Hideo's

death reignites this rivalry, providing a chance to settle the score.

**Motivation.** The player must navigate their desire for revenge while uncovering the Samurai's

connection to Hideo. Along the way, they discover a deeper purpose to Hideo's death, forcing

them to choose between vengeance and understanding.

##### Seeking the Soulsteel Forge

Master Hideo's forge, known for producing unparalleled Soulsteel blades, is rumored to hold

secrets about the craft. The players--whether smiths, warriors, or seekers of knowledge--are

drawn to Nagano to learn from the master's legacy.

**Motivation.** At the forge, the players find clues pointing to a final, unfinished blade and cryptic

messages hinting at Hideo's purpose. The path to completing the weapon leads to the Ryuseki

Peaks and the Twice-Cursed Samurai.


##### The Jackal's Challenge

At the funeral, a jackal emissary of the Twice-Cursed Samurai appears and challenges the

players directly:

```
"Master Hideo's fate is sealed, but his path is not yet finished. Seek the peaks if you wish
```
```
to know the truth."
```
**Motivation.** The players are intrigued or insulted by the jackal's message. Whether to uncover

the Samurai's role in Hideo's death, prove their strength, or learn more about the master's

legacy, they set off toward the Ryuseki Peaks.

##### A Broken Spirit

A local shrine keeper or wandering priest tells the players of strange spiritual disturbances near

Nagano. The kami grow restless, and the yokai become bold, whispering of a fractured soul

lingering in the mortal world.

**Motivation.** The players are drawn to uncover the spiritual imbalance, which leads them to the

Twice-Cursed Samurai and the nature of Hideo's fractured soul. Resolving this disturbance

becomes a test of their honor and spiritual strength.

#### Using the Hooks

Each hook connects to the central narrative while giving players a unique and personal reason

to engage with the adventure. You can:

1. Provide multiple hooks for players to choose from.
2. Combine hooks for a more layered and complex motivation.
3. Use the Jackal's Challenge as a unifying event to tie individual motivations into the larger

```
story.
```

## Adventure: The Twice Cursed- ACT I - Master

## Hideo's Funeral

#### The Funeral of Master Hideo

The adventure starts at a serene cemetery outside Nagano, where Master Hideo's funeral is

taking place. Players are pulled into the enigma surrounding Hideo's demise and the meaning

behind his sacrifice.

#### Key Events

```
? Strange Hints: During the funeral, hushed conversations among villagers and Hideo's
```

#### The Prayer Ceremony

##### Disruption

The serene atmosphere of the prayer ceremony is disrupted by the arrival of Lady Hana Arashi,

a concubine of Daimyo Ryoko. Accompanied by samurai and retainers, her presence is

noticeable, yet respectful. The mourners react with surprise and uncertainty to her arrival.

##### Offering

Lady Hana approaches the shrine and presents a small iron crane figurine, a symbol of the

Arashi clan's respect for Hideo. She addresses the gathering, expressing condolences on behalf

of Daimyo Ryoko.

##### Reactions

The villagers and nobles are unsure of Lady Hana's intentions, questioning whether her

presence is a genuine gesture or a political maneuver. The players have various options on how

to react to her presence and words.

#### Yokai and the Raven Yokai

Yokai are spiritual beings connected to nature and the supernatural. They can be good, bad, or

neutral depending on the situation and the balance of the natural world.

Ravens are seen as messengers of omens, often linked to death, secrets, and wisdom. The

appearance of a Raven Yokai at a sacred place like the Whispering Glade is rare and important,

likely indicating a spiritual imbalance.

#### Political Landscape and the Impact of Hideo's Death

**Arashi Clan's Dominance:** The Arashi clan, led by the pragmatic Daimyo Ryoko Arashi, holds

significant regional power, overseeing several minor lords and prioritizing order.

**Lord Kagemura's Ambitions:** Lord Ichiro Kagemura, a minor noble under the Arashi clan,

harbors ambitions that outweigh his resources. He manages a small fief near the Frostwood.

**Power Vacuum and Potential for Instability:** Hideo's passing has created a power vacuum in

Nagano. Although not a political figure, his influence maintained stability. Factions like the

Arashi clan may exploit this situation to expand their control.

## Funeral Setting and Traditions

The funeral takes place in the Whispering Glade, a sacred area within the Frostwood known for

the faint whispers believed to be the voices of spirits. This location holds significance for both

mourning and spiritual practices, serving as a venue for important rituals and ceremonies.

Monks oversee the ceremony, offering prayers to Tsuchi-no-Megumi to guide Hideo's spirit to

rest. Attendees are expected to offer tokens of remembrance, such as tools, stones, or blades.

#### Underlying Tensions


Despite the gathering to honor Hideo, an atmosphere of unease permeates the event due to

political rivalries, fear of yokai, and suspicion surrounding the circumstances of his death.


## Adventure: The Twice Cursed - ACT II - Through the

## Jade Forest

#### Journey Through the Hisui no Mori

The players navigate the enchanted Hisui no Mori (Jade Forest) on their way to the Ryuseki

Peaks. The forest is teeming with magic, spirits, and perils.

#### 1. Forest Exploration

The Hisui no Mori is a dynamic labyrinth, presenting puzzles, traps, and encounters that

challenge the players' determination and ingenuity.

**Forest Features:**

```
? Shifting paths, illusions, and mischievous yokai.
```
```
? Sacred Shrines where players can seek guidance or protection from the kami.
```
```
? Dangerous creatures like shadowy kappa (river spirits) or a roaming Oni (ogre spirit).
```
#### 2. Encounters with the Jackals of the Twice-Cursed

The Samurai's jackals are present throughout the forest, observing the players or leading them

astray with riddles and challenges.

**Example Encounter:**

```
? A jackal disguised as a traveling monk offers assistance but tests the players' honor. If
```
```
they fail, the monk reveals their true nature and summons yokai to harass them.
```
#### 3. Reaching the Threshold of the Peaks

At the forest's edge, the players discover an ancient gate guarded by a spectral samurai who

challenges them with a duel or a riddle. The gate leads into the Ryuseki Peaks, where the

Twice-Cursed Samurai resides.


## Act II: Journey Through the Hisui no Mori

#### Summary

In Act II, the players embark on a perilous journey into the Hisui no Mori (Jade Forest) to track

the clues left in the wake of Master Hideo's funeral. The forest, a living maze imbued with magic

and inhabited by kami and yokai, tests their strength, wits, and honor. The players must uncover

the forest's secrets, survive its trials, and locate the entrance to the Ryuseki Peaks, where the

Twice-Cursed Samurai awaits.

#### Goals for Act II

1. **Track the Clues.** Follow the trail of the missing artifact, the Raven Yokai's feather, or the

```
mysterious tracks leading into the forest.
```
2. **Navigate the Hisui no Mori.** Overcome natural hazards, spiritual barriers, and shifting

```
paths.
```
3. **Face the Trials of the Forest.** Engage with kami and yokai through puzzles, combat, or

```
diplomacy to earn safe passage.
```
4. **Find the Gate to the Ryuseki Peaks.** Solve the mystery of the spectral samurai

```
guarding the ancient gate at the forest's edge.
```
#### I. Setting Out (Afternoon of Day 1)

**Objective.** The players begin their journey into the Jade Forest. They must decide which trail to

follow based on the clues they've uncovered.

##### Key Details

The forest is dense and alive with an unnatural hum of magic. Tracks lead deeper into the

woods, accompanied by faint spiritual energy that resonates with the Raven Yokai's feather or

the missing artifact.

##### Encounters

```
? Tracks to Follow. Players must succeed on a Survival (DC 12) or Perception (DC 15)
```
```
check to follow the trail. Failure causes them to become disoriented, losing time or
```
```
encountering a yokai (a mischievous Kitsune that leads them astray).
```
```
? Mournful Kami. The players encounter a small shrine guarded by a sorrowful (Lesser
```
```
Earth Kami). The kami offers vague warnings:
```
```
? "The forest remembers. The forest watches. Tread lightly, for the Twice-Cursed
```
```
stirs its soul."
```

```
? Players may gain blessings (advantage on Survival checks) if they honor the
```
```
kami with an offering or prayer.
```
#### II. Shifting Paths (Evening of Day 1)

**Objective.** The forest begins to disorient the players with illusions and magical disturbances.

#### Key Details

The Hisui no Mori twists and reshapes itself, creating a living maze. The players must pass a

skill challenge (Skill Challenge: Navigating the Living Maze of Hisui no Mori) to navigate the

shifting paths, with the risk of wandering into dangerous areas.

##### Encounters:

```
? The Spirit Lanterns: Floating lanterns marked with symbols lead in multiple directions.
```
```
? A History (DC 13) or Religion (DC 15) check reveals the symbol aligned with Hideo's
```
```
teachings, leading the players closer to their destination.
```
```
? Failure attracts a hostile Shikigami or other forest guardians (5x Kodama or 1x
```
```
Jorogumo).
```
```
? Hidden Yokai Ambush: A Stealth (DC 14) or Insight (DC 12) check allows the players to
```
```
spot a group of yokai (3x Shadow Wolf).
```
```
? Failure results in an ambush and a skirmish or retreat.
```
#### III. The Trials of the Forest (Night of Day 1)

**Objective.** The players face spiritual and physical trials to earn the forest's favor or continue

their journey.

#### Key Details

The forest's kami manifest to test the players' resolve, wisdom, and honor. Success earns

blessings or safe passage, while failure results in consequences like lost time or combat.

##### Encounters

###### The Trial of Wits

The players find themselves in a glade where their reflections speak to them, offering cryptic

riddles.

Solving the riddle correctly reveals the way forward.

```
"I was born in fire, shaped by earth, and tested by water. What am I?" (Answer: Steel)
```

Failing the riddles causes the glade to erupt into hostile illusions, requiring saving throws to

avoid exhaustion or disorientation.

###### The Trial of Honor

A kami disguised as a wandering warrior challenges the party to prove their honor. (Samurai

Fighter (Level 5 NPC)). Players must engage in a non-lethal duel (with controlled combat

mechanics) or demonstrate their integrity through dialogue.

#### IV. The Threshold of the Ryuseki Peaks (Morning of Day 2)

**Objective.** The players reach the forest's edge and confront the spectral guardian of the ancient

gate leading to the Ryuseki Peaks.

##### Key Details

The gate is carved into a stone outcropping, flanked by torii arches and glowing with faint

spiritual energy.

A Spectral Samurai emerges, its voice echoing with both warning and challenge:

```
"None may pass who are unworthy. Steel your hearts and face the shadows of your
```
```
souls. Will you be tested by steel or by soul?
```
##### Encounters

**Combat Challenge.** The players must battle the spectral samurai, who tests their skill and unity.

Weakened Spectral Samurai

**Puzzle Alternative.** The gate contains a riddle or puzzle tied to Hideo's teachings. Solving it

causes the spectral samurai to kneel and open the gate without combat. The Gate of Master

Hideo

```
```

```
? Prioritize Combat. Treat the forest's trials as a gauntlet of challenges, focusing on
```
```
survival and strength.
```
```
? Explore the Mysteries. Unravel clues about Hideo's death and the Twice-Cursed
```
```
Samurai through investigation and careful observation.
```

## Act III: The Labyrinth of the Twice-Cursed

#### The Ryuseki Peaks: A Realm of the Twice-Cursed Samurai

The Ryuseki Peaks exist as a surreal domain where the influence of the Twice-Cursed Samurai

warps reality itself. This area blends traditional dungeon-crawling mechanics with elements of

high fantasy exploration.

##### Navigating the Peaks

The peaks are fraught with danger, characterized by steep cliffs, icy winds, and paths that twist

back on themselves, creating a disorienting experience.

```
? Encounters:
```
```
? Mountain Yokai: Spectral tengu or animated stone guardians fueled by the
```
```
Samurai's power.
```
```
? Planar Distortions: The peaks are infused with chaotic energy, causing
```
```
unpredictable shifts in time and space.
```
##### The Labyrinthine Domain

Hidden within the peaks lies the Twice-Cursed Samurai's fortress, a constantly changing

labyrinth of stone, shadow, and glass.

```
? Key Features:
```
```
? The Blade Room: A vast chamber filled with shattered Soulsteel blades,
```
```
remnants of the Samurai's cursed past.
```
```
? The Hall of Reflections: A disorienting space filled with mirrors, where players
```
```
confront illusions of themselves and their loved ones.
```
```
? The Throne of the Twice-Cursed: The Samurai's seat of power, a circular
```
```
chamber where the final battle unfolds.
```
## The Climax: Confrontation with the Twice-Cursed

## Samurai

#### Encounter: The Twice-Cursed Samurai

**1. The Samurai's Arrival:**

```
? The Twice-Cursed Samurai, a figure radiating charisma and otherworldliness, makes
```
```
their entrance. They present a blend of menace and charm, engaging the players with
```

```
riddles and cryptic insights regarding Master Hideo's sacrifice.
```
**2. The Honorable Duel:**

```
? The Samurai challenges the players to a duel that tests not only their physical prowess
```
```
but also their wisdom and unity.
```
```
? Unique Mechanics:
```
```
? The Samurai's attacks inflict spiritual damage, temporarily weakening the players'
```
```
resolve (e.g., reducing Wisdom or Charisma).
```
```
? The battleground shifts and changes throughout the encounter, forcing the
```
```
players to adjust to new terrain and illusions.
```
**3. Resolution: Defeat or Surrender**

```
? Upon Defeat: The Twice-Cursed Samurai reveals the truth: Hideo orchestrated his own
```
```
death to become a guardian spirit for his grandson. The Samurai, bound by their curse,
```
```
honored Hideo's wishes but grapples with the repercussions.
```
```
? Upon Surrender: The Samurai may yield with honor, offering their cursed blade to the
```
```
players as a symbol of respect.
```
#### The Ryuseki Peaks (Exploration)

The Ryuseki Peaks are foreboding and alien. The air is thin, the wind howls, and the landscape

seems to bend under the strain of planar distortions. Paths loop back on themselves, time flows

inconsistently, and the peaks are populated by both spiritual guardians and yokai corrupted by

the Samurai's curse.

##### Encounters in the Ryuseki Peaks

```
Trap: Shifting Cairns
```
```
Encounter: Mountain Yokai
```
```
? Enemies: Ghostly Lesser Tengu or animated stone guardians
```
```
? Purpose: Test the players' resolve
```
```
? Tengu Tactics: Hit-and-run attacks from above, using the terrain to their advantage
```
**Challenge: Planar Distortions**

```
? Description: Areas with warped reality, causing time skips and gravity shifts
```
```
? Skills: Survival (DC 13), Arcana (DC 14), or Athletics (DC 12)
```
```
? Failure: Disorientation (exhaustion or damage) or temporary party separation
```
**Encounter: The Bridge of Wavering Shadows**


```
? Description: Ethereal bridge guarded by a spectral yokai demanding a toll
```
```

```
```

```
```
(Answer: Words)
```
```
? Offer a personal sacrifice or valuable item
```
```
? Pass a Persuasion (DC 15) or Insight (DC 14) check to understand the yokai's
```
```
motives
```
##### The Labyrinthine Domain (Dungeon)

**Description.** Deep within the peaks lies the fortress of the Twice-Cursed Samurai. The labyrinth

is a surreal and shifting maze that reflects the Samurai's fractured spirit and cursed existence.

##### Key Rooms and Encounters

###### The Blade Room

```
? What it is: A room filled with broken Soulsteel blades, each holding pieces of the
```
```
Samurai's memories.
```
```
? The Challenge: Players must reconstruct the Samurai's story by collecting blade
```
```
fragments and interpreting their whispers using Arcana, Insight, or History checks.
```
```
? The Danger: Touching the wrong blade inflicts psychic damage or brings a cursed shard
```
```
to life, attacking the players.
```
###### The Hall of Reflections

```
? What it is: A hall of mirrors that conjures illusions of the players' deepest regrets or
```
```
fears.
```
```
? The Challenge: Players must distinguish reality from illusion and resist mental
```
```
manipulation through Wisdom saving throws to avoid being paralyzed or charmed, and
```
```
Perception or Investigation checks to find the right path.
```
```
? The Danger: Failure forces players to face hostile mirror copies of themselves.
```
###### The Chamber of Echoes

```
? What it is: A large, circular room with walls that distort the players' voices into
```
```
accusations or taunts.
```
```
? The Challenge: Solve a sound-based puzzle by either harmonizing through a group
```
```
Performance check or deciphering the true meaning of the distorted words using Insight.
```
#### The Throne of the Twice-Cursed (Climax)

```
Setting: A vast, circular chamber of black stone, illuminated by an ethereal red glow. The
```
```
Samurai's imposing throne, a structure of jagged metal and shattered glass, stands at the
```

```
center.
```
##### Phase 1: The Samurai's Entrance

```
The Twice-Cursed Samurai, a figure of menace and sorrow, confronts the players with
```
```
cryptic riddles and philosophical questions about honor, sacrifice, and Hideo's legacy. They
```
```
may offer the players a chance to leave, testing their courage.
```
```
Example Dialogue:
```
```
? "Master Hideo's death was no tragedy but a gift. Do you understand why?"
```
```
? "You seek answers, but are you ready for the weight of truth? Or will you crumble, as I
```
```
did?"
```
##### Phase 2: The Duel

```
The Samurai challenges the players to a battle that tests their strength, wisdom, and unity.
```
```
Unique Mechanics:
```
```
? Fractured Spirit: The Samurai's attacks inflict both physical and spiritual harm. Each hit
```
```
forces a DC 13 Charisma Saving Throw or the target suffers temporary Charisma or
```
```
Wisdom damage.
```
```
? Shifting Arena: The battlefield changes at the end of each round:
```
```
? Round 2: Illusions obscure parts of the battlefield, requiring Perception Checks
```
```
(DC 14) to target the Samurai.
```
```
? Round 3: The ground fractures into floating platforms, requiring Athletics or
```
```
Acrobatics Checks (DC 12) to move without falling.
```
###### Spectral Samurai Abilities (Adjusted for Challenge)

```
? Multiattack with Soul Blade: Deals slashing and necrotic damage.
```
```
? Phantom Strike (Recharge 5-6): Teleports and attacks all nearby enemies.
```
```
? Challenge of Honor: Marks a single player for additional damage and advantage.
```
##### Phase 3: Resolution

**Samurai's Defeat.** If defeated, the Samurai kneels and reveals the truth: "Master Hideo's death

was my greatest act of honor--and my greatest curse." They confess that Hideo orchestrated

his own death to become a guardian spirit for his grandson and protect Shuigari.

**Samurai's Surrender.** If the players defeat the Samurai honorably or display great wisdom,

they may convince them to surrender willingly. The Samurai offers their cursed blade as a sign

of respect--a powerful weapon with both great potential and a hidden cost.


**Revelation.** The players learn the truth of Hideo's sacrifice and the Samurai's role in ensuring

the boy's survival.

## grey-tide

The Grey Tide
Campaign Setting: Shuigari Campaign: Shuigari, The Last Whisper of Steel

Around Takashima

Primary Operations
* Smuggling: The Grey Tide uses hidden coves near Takashima's coastlines, particularly in the rugged cliffs west of the Iron Valley, to stash goods.
    * Contraband includes Frostiron, stolen artifacts, and enchanted items.
* Piracy: Merchant ships leaving Takashima City along the Silver Road's maritime routes are frequent targets.
Local Reputation
* Among Villagers: Coastal villages near Takashima sometimes provide the Grey Tide with food or shelter in exchange for protection from rival pirates or bandits. Some view them as folk heroes, while others resent their exploitation.
* With Nobles: Nobles in Takashima City, especially Lady Ryoko Arashi, see the Grey Tide as a nuisance undermining their control of trade and resources.
Recent Activity
Increased Tension: Rumors suggest the Grey Tide recently raided a shipment of enchanted blades bound for Takashima City, heightening military crackdowns in the region. Some believe they may have intercepted one of Hideo's final creations.
Spiritual Disturbances: Fishermen claim the Grey Tide angered a sea kami during a smuggling operation, leading to increased yokai sightings near the coast.
Potential Hooks for the Campaign:
* A Grey Tide captain might attend Hideo's funeral in disguise, scouting potential allies or rivals among the mourners.
* The Grey Tide may hold information about the stolen ceremonial katana or other artifacts tied to Hideo's legacy.
* A covert meeting between a Takashima noble and a Grey Tide representative could hint at secret alliances or betrayals.
* There is a low ranking bandit of the grey tide that Hideo once helped escape the local lord's samurai.

## lord-daizen

Lord Daizen Kuroda
